Theorem 217.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: The organic operator L associated with convergence analysis theory satisfies the maximum principle with constant 425. Its spectrum is contained in the disk of radius 958 with 3 eigenvalues on the boundary.

Proof: By the convergence analysis principle applied to organic theory, we decompose the problem into 425 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the convergence analysis fixed-point theorem with constant 958. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(3/n). QED.

Each subproblem has a solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 4. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result. QED.

Theorem 217.3 (Optimality Condition)

Statement: For any organic module M satisfying the optimality condition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 349. The projective resolution has length at most 678 with Betti numbers bounded by 2.

Proof: We apply the optimality condition principle to the organic decomposition. By minimality, the element a^349 must satisfy the required bound, contradicting our assumption. As a consequence, the 678-dimensional case follows by the same argument. QED.
